Election of candidate or candidate for the coverage of a vacancy of a member of the Galician Board of Auditors Election of candidates for the coverage of three vacancies of elected members of the Galicia Advisory Council
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u2	We go into the agenda with the election points. We'll do it together. In this case the election to cover a vacancy of the Galician Board of Auditors and also the election of candidates to cover three vacancies of the Galician Advisory Council. For the election of a member of the Council of Auditors and of three members of the Consultative Council of Galicia, the deadlines for the submission of proposals for candidates or candidates admitted to the procedure were completed, and the perceptual appearances in Commission 1st, Institutional, General Administration, Justice and Internal, at the meeting of 11 December, the Bureau and the Speakers' Board agreed to the inclusion in the agenda of the plenary for their election. The election, in accordance with Article 91 1.2 and (2) of the Galician Parliament Regulation, shall be secreted, by means of papers and by alphabetical order of the surname. In both cases candidates will be elected if they get a majority of three fifths, i.e. 45 votes in first ballot, and, if so, an absolute majority in second ballot for the alleged election of the members of the Consultative Council of Galicia. The white papers are for the candidate of the Board of Auditors, and green for those of the Galician Advisory Council. These include the names of the proposed candidates and the proposed parliamentary group. The vote shall be taken as follows: the votes in favour shall be taken by inserting into the ballot boxes previously delivered by the services of the House in which the name of the candidate proposed for the Council of Auditors and three names proposed for the Consultative Council of Galicia -- marking the corresponding box and boxes with a stamp -- ; blank votes shall be when no name is indicated; null votes shall be made in papers other than those approved by the Bureau, or in which the name or names of other persons are entered, or which contain scraps or signals other than those used to grant the vote to the candidates proposed. So let's start. I suppose you already have the white paper for the Board of Auditors -- not yet, they're sharing -- and the green paper for the Advisory Council. When this process is over, the secretary, Mrs. Corina Porro, will make the call in alphabetical order. I pray to the vice president first and to the vice-secretary lady that the ballot boxes are on. [[The House's services continue to distribute the ballots.]] I remind you that, even if you put the name -- I think I said it -- you have to mark it with a dash.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u3	Have they all voted yet?
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u4	The result of the vote for the Board of Auditors is as follows: the votes cast are 73; votes in favour of Mr Simón Rego Vilar, 73. Thus, having reached most of the three fifths with a glance, it is elected as a member of the Board of Auditors by Simon Rego Vilar. And for the Advisory Council, the votes cast are 73. The votes in favour of Don José Luís Costa Pillado are 73; votes in favour of Don Ignacio de Loyola Aranguren Pérez, 73; and votes in favour of Don Alberto Souto Souto, 73. As a result, the majority of the three fifths are also elected to the Consultative Council of Galicia by Don José Luís Costa Pillado, Mr Ignacio by Loyola Aranguren Pérez and Mr Alberto Souto Souto.

Debates and votes by Ditame, drawn up by Commission 2nd, Territorial Planning, Public Works, Environment and Services, on the draft Continental Fisheries Act of Galicia
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u6	Amendments to the Socialist Parliamentary Group and to the Galician Nationalist Block Parliamentary Group are maintained. For the presentation of the award, the President of Commission 2nd, Territorial Order, Public Works, Environment and Services, Ms Carmen Pomar Tojo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u7	Thank you very much, President. Good morning, gentlemen. The Galician Continental Fisheries Act was entered into the Parliamentary Register on 24 September 2020 and its admission to the procedure was carried out by the Parliamentary Bureau at its meeting on 29 September, opening the deadline for the submission of amendments, which ended on 19 October and 2 November 2020 for amendments to the whole and the joint. In particular, the amendments to the full refund submitted by the parliamentary groups of the Galician Nationalist Bloc and the Galician Socialists were rejected at the plenary meeting on 10 November 2020. And the amendments to the articulate presented and admitted to the procedure were a total of 106 -- 89 of the Parliamentary Group of the Socialists of Galicia and 17 of the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block of Galicia --. Commission 2nd, Territorial Planning, Public Works, Environment and Services, at its meeting on 13 November, designated the presentation to prepare the report which was formed on that date. And at the following four meetings, the rapporteur and the keynote speakers -- Ms Marisol Díaz Mouteira, by the Galician People's Parliamentary Group; Mr Xosé Luís Rivas Cruz, by the Galego Nationalist Block Parliamentary Group; and Mr Pablo Arangüena Fernández, by the Galician Socialist Group -- prepared the report with the final recommendations, which were issued on 25 November. Subsequently, Commission 2nd, Territorial Planning, Public Works, Environment and Services, at its meeting on 11 November, approved the dictation I am presenting. It is therefore necessary to complete the processing of this legislative procedure today with the approval, if any, of the Galician Continental Fisheries Act. Thank you very much.

Besides it has also allowed us to check the coincidences in this matter with the BNG, represented by Mr. Xosé Luís Rivas, who shares the view of fishing as a defense of the river environment, the difference of the environmental advisor; or, as you can tell, of the administrative-fluvial bureaucracy of the Galician Union; or of the destruction of Galician PP by the environment and by the fish richness they once had and no longer have the rivers of this country -- that Cunqueriro said they were a thousand --. The rivers of Galicia are much more than a thousand, because each small valley has its little watercourse. It's a wealth in terms of comparison with other places in the world, in addition to the extent of our river network, by its primitive variety, which has dramatically rubbed out over the last forty years, an authentic wealth -- as I say -- to which Galicia lives on her back, not knowing it and radically led by an autonomous government that despises how much she ignores. And recognizing the good manners and manners of Mrs. Díaz Mouteira, I also thank you for incorporating some of the approximately 90 amendments that the Socialist Group has tabled. But I'm sorry it was just the least transcendental. Because, for example, to change the object of the law came to a compromise between the three political forces that was quite difficult to achieve, which gives an idea of the poor flexibility of the PP, because it cost a lot even to introduce the environmental issue into the object of a law that in 1992 had it. And that sums up everything. The law of 1992 put forward progress. This is a reversal despite the twenty-eight years in which the world has become much more concerned and concerned about the environment. But the PP goes backwards, even with regard to that law of 1992, which was made when environmental law was in leather. And you started by eliminating the environmental content of that law's object to replace it with a ethereal and legally inadequate "right to the exercise of continental fishing." It remains very modern and falsely progressive to call it "right" to which all life has been, in technical and legal terms, a special common use of a public good like the rivers. But if there's no river ecosystem, there's no fishing. And if there's no fishing, there's no right to fishing, no catfish rags, because there's no trout, no rites, no salmon. That is why I must reiterate that it is unheard of that a law made in the year 2020, which, in theory, aims to protect and encourage the continental fishing of our community, in practice implies a genuine retreat in that protection and promotion, as a law made significantly worse in 1992. It's also unnecessary, because there's no real need for a new fishing law, except for the fact that the Environment Department shows very little normative fertility. I mean, since the Environment Department has been without significant legislative activity for some time, a law had to be made. And this was the case, an unfortunate and unfortunate occurrence, an unnecessary legal occurrence that lacks justification, since in the explanatory statement the long vigil of the 1992 law is used as the main argument, when the problem is not the long vigil of that norm, but the problem is the very mismanagement of the resources carried out by the Galician Union, the constant omission of its competences and obligations, namely, to sanction and prevent the aggressions of rivers and the constant environmental deterioration of river ecosystems, which has resulted in a situation of abandonment of river fishing by many of its practitioners. Almost half the number of fishing licenses fell in the last decade: from about 90,000 to about 45,000; nothing to do with the generational relief, as I disagreed with the explanatory statement, and anything to do with the impossibility of fishing half a dozen trouts where eleven years ago it was possible to do it. I myself inherited the passion for my father's river fishing and for 20 years I practiced that condition while I was seeing how river ecosystems and fishing were less than in Galicia, to a point where a fisherman like me ceases to draw the license, ceases to go to the river, because there are not enough trout to practice fishing. It's not overfishing, it's because we fishermen are getting less and less. As I said earlier in this plenary, there's no fishing because a river is an ecosystem, and from that ecosystem, salmonites whose fishing has been part of our ancestral culture for centuries. And not only our own but also others, because trout and salmon fishing is a condition shared by tens of millions of people in the world. And Galicia has one of Spain's largest catch concentrations, and Spain is the first country in the European Union in number of prisoners and the fifth in the world. The country of the thousand rivers of Cunqueiro has become the country of the hundreds of dams, and each dam is an obstacle to the upstream of the salmonids, which tend to climb up the rivers to find their mills in the high courses of the rivers or in the beds of the rivers. If we cut them back and down by interposing the wall of a hydropower dam that can't clear, they beat their populations -- not to mention that they can't come down once they defuse --. And they die if the river becomes dry for failure to comply with the ecological flow, as repeatedly allows the Union of Galicia. And what affects trout is even more affected by salmon and sharks, which are migratory species. And you not only allowed and sheltered all kinds of non-compliances from the dealers, but they favor those same companies in any way possible. Then there's the problem of pollution, which, instead of going a little less, goes further; among other things, because you don't sanction much or little, you don't sanction except witness to be able to say that you sanction. And there is also the problem of the deterioration of river banks and erosion and the deterioration of river forests, which, in addition to forming part of the river ecosystem, when it rains, are those that capture part of that rain -- about 70% -- that return to the atmosphere by evapotransspiration, while the remaining 30% goes to the river and then to the sea. But when the forests of the river are damaged or burned, evapotranspiration is reduced and then there are more and more intense and harmful floods, such as those we see too often in Galicia. So the problem with river fishing is that river ecosystems are increasingly degraded, and without ecosystems there is no food or habitat for fishable species. The problem with this law is that it's going to go to history because it's incredibly a less environmentalist law than the one Fraga made. This law makes it disappear as a serious infringement of the breach of the ecological flow that was in the past, or the failure to comply with the obligation to install and maintain in good condition lift devices. It provides only requirements for new hydraulic concessions and authorisations without facing the failure to comply with the numerous previous concessions and authorisations which constantly omit obligations established to sustain ecosystems, such as the installation of dams and protective devices to prevent access of icticicle population to drift currents or near currents, or the already mentioned installation of upstream devices or other alternatives to ensure periodic migration of fish along river courses or maintain minimal flow flows, ecological flows. These are matters that remain without being properly incorporated into the text after the legislative process in commission, which indicates that in this field the Government of Greece is moving incredibly towards a much more lax regulation with all that it can provoke, and does not introduce remarkable news for the control of hazardous landfills and wastes that affect aquatic fauna and water. It also greatly reduces the sanctioning regime as it lowers the amount of sanctions for offenders, thereby reducing the coercive power of the norm. In general, infringements are reduced by less than half, when twenty-eight years have passed and the simple effect of inflation accumulated in twenty-eight years should cause it to multiply. The inflation accumulated since 1992 is 88 %. That is, a penalty of 100 euros, or the corresponding pesetas of then, in 1992, if we updated it, would today be equivalent to a penalty of 188 euros -- 100 euros in 1992, 188 euros in this year --. This means that sanctions would almost have to be doubled by the simple effect of inflation, and yet you reduce them generally to half. For example, the very serious infringement penalty, which could reach 50 million in the law of 1992, is now up to EUR 150,000 -- half -- and so on. It's half that in 1992, and so -- I insist -- successively. A discharge which is considered to be a very serious infringement, for example, leaves today at half the price that in 1992. If we count inflation, it comes out even for the fourth part of the price. We're going back, like crabs -- by the way, not like autochthonous river crabs, which are practically extinct -- and we're moving from the principle of "who pollutes pay" to the principle of "who pollutes pays much less than what he paid before this law." No clear measures are also laid down for the conservation of beds which guarantee the maintenance of their natural conditions. And another fundamental aspect that does not foresee the bill is the problem that is generating the limited number of troops for inspection and surveillance work, which are almost null in the summer's time, because most of the troops are destined for fire prevention and extinction work. Therefore, this law does not establish any novelty relating to this important circumstance, even if it is satisfied that there will be no financial increase in these matters from the moment when the law can enter into force. Nor can we accept as a valid justification the need for this rule to encourage the generational renewal of the group of river fishermen and the promotion of the activity of continental fishing as a tool for tourism development in rural areas, since there has been the possibility of acting through other non-lawless standards that have not been developed to achieve these goals and that are now trying to present as important novelties of this law. For years, acts such as the authorizations of new floating equipment, new fishing gears, river schools or non-licension for minors have been put in place, and the exemption from the rate for those over 65. And no protective measures are established in the coastal forests. So that if this law does not conceive rivers as ecosystems but as places for the exercise of fiction, impossible or almost impossible, right to fishing without anything to fish! Riverwood is fundamental and not treated in this law. In short, the 1992 fishing law was a necessary law, which called for progress in regulating river fishing in Galicia, to the extent that it surpassed a franchising law that had been overcome by half a century of changes in river fishing and in the conservation of the environment. This law involved a retreat before passing through this House and continues to involve a retreat today. In any case, Counsellor, with the same fishing law you can do very different things depending on the desire to defend and promote fishing in Galicia. We hope that the Environmental Adviser will have something better to do for our rivers than a campaign photo throwing trout pods into the Anllóns River, a river with enormous potential, which, like all our rivers, the rivers of Galicia, needs conservation measures, not their photos or trout pods. Nothing else and we'll go on afterwards. Thank you very much.

We have to give clear answers and follow evolution. It is clear that what took 40 years ago because it was a fishing practice to sometimes help as a source of food today is being a recreational and sporting activity, and it will agree with a greater social awareness about the need to protect our natural resources. So it reflects the project in its main object: regulate, protect, promote the right to exercise fishing in continental waters and an orderly use of fish resources. This instrument is supported by four key pillars: the implementation of measures to ensure generational change; promoting environmental training and education and awareness-raising in this area; promoting fishing without death; also using fishing as a tool for tourism, economic and social development in rural areas; and, of course, the fourth pillar, strategic planning of continental fisheries management in order to optimize resource management. Ladies and gentlemen, the average profile of the Greek fishermen focuses on an age range between 50 and 70, but with a very significant percentage in the stretch ranging from 70 to 80. It is undoubtedly true that our population, especially in some provinces, has a marked old character, but it is no less true also that it is essential to encourage the participation of younger generations in order to ensure the future of this sport practice. And, above all -- let's not forget -- you focus a lot on sanctioning -- which, of course, I will also clarify -- but I also want to focus on that essential task of raising awareness, improving that environmental awareness, especially among the younger ones. They really are the ones who have the obligation to preserve our river ecosystems. It introduces important news, such as the creation of river schools, that tool that will help to form or even -- why not say -- to foster that intergenerational encounter among those who are used to fishing today and those that we want to try to motivate and encourage. In addition, we want to attract young people who make it easier from the administrative point of view. On the one hand, Mr. Aranguena says that there is excessive bureaucracy; and on the other hand, he asks for protection. Let's look for the balance, which is really the objective of this law. Children under the age of 14 may practise sport or recreational fishing without a license when accompanied by an adult. I think it's a novelty of this law to be highlighted. And all minors will be exempt from payment of fees. I think you can also encourage sports practice. By the way, that exemption will also benefit those over 65. Of course, this free fishing permit for older people in any case will be for deathless fishing, one of the fundamental objectives that we want to promote in this law. Several measures are included for the progressive implementation of deathless fishing, which we know is not the common in our community, and that is undoubtedly one of our challenges. In spite of the fact that fishing without death is gaining followers and it is the common rule all over the world, the intention in this case in this law and in our community is that this kind of activity can be promoted by coexisting with the traditional extractive model. We want -- where I would also like to make fincapé -- to leave a special protection for certain traditional fishing activities that are representative of the Greek culture, such as the Ulla River fisheries or the Tea River stakes for catching the lamprea. We know that fishing is closely linked to the population in rural areas and that is why we bet it is an instrument that plays a key role in tourism, economic and social development in these areas. It was precisely the third piar that I mentioned earlier, which was the subject of hesitation in the debates, as they moved me, and we understand that too, because it obviously goes in every political project of each of you. I'm referring to private fishing waters, a figure that will certainly help boost fishing tourism, as you are doing it in other points in other autonomous communities, such as in Castile and León. The private fishing arrangements in this intensive scheme regulated by the law will also serve as elements to encourage fishing for the above-mentioned programmes with children, for the practice of fishing by the larger ones, and we believe that it will facilitate the training of the competitors and competitors in Greece, which will also move the fishing economy in periods of ban. Rural people, no doubt, want to be one of the main beneficiaries. And it ends at the fourth pier, which is nothing other than strategic planning of continental fisheries management in order to optimize resource management. To this end, three instruments are created: the Greek Continental Fisheries Management Plan, the technical fisheries management plans -- whether by shells or subconcaves -- and the annual continental fisheries order. We don't understand it's a turnaround or an aesthetic reform. We really believe that silent proposals are coming up. For example, water masses are classified as fishable and non-fishable, and can be the first free for fishing or subject to a special regime. In particular, this special scheme is regulated in four categories: fishing costs, sections of water of particular interest to fish wealth and more novelties, the already mentioned sports and training scenarios and the fishing waters of private use, those which, as I mentioned, emerged from controversy. What else incorporates the law? For it incorporates a list of species that may be the object of continental fishing, regulates invasive exotic species, adopts specific control and capture measures of the species catalogued, establishing the clear prohibition of these species once captured. There are, by the way, so unique news that there is no such thing as Europe. I am talking, for example, about the establishment of a self-guided system, or identification of catches for the specimens of offences, so that it will be the fisherman himself who communicates to the administrations the data and the catches made, a system that will simplify the controls for the catch of this species, but, above all, to make the fisherman responsible for the management. If there is news in the criminal conduct and sanctions: the classifications of the categories of infringements are simplified, remove the category of less serious and reduce the amount when the offender recognizes his or her responsibility or performs the payment in a voluntary period. It must be noted that special attention is devoted to repairing the damage caused and restoring the situation altered to its previous state, which seems to me to be very defensive aspects of course. We stand out here -- which seems to have been ridiculed -- the result of the commission. I believe that all political groups applauded what was the amendment to article 1 when it was given greater weight -- let's say -- to the concept "conservation", which is already introduced into the article of the law. It seemed to me that this looked like something positive, but I think it's best sometimes to take the opposite because it doesn't come out of the argumentary and we can pipe, and call ourselves four hundred. I believe that the spirit of conservation was implicit and explicit in every law, but with that attitude of reaching agreements and seeking consensus, in which we understand that it is an important law, they accepted that amendment. And I was saying my partner, since Mr. Arunguena repeated several times -- he did it in the Commission and in the first intervention -- that he was no longer a fisherman, that one of the challenges was to get him back out of the fishing licence. It's probably not the law's fault that it isn't, but in any case it's also a challenge of this law. They all quoted a Mindonian writer, as was Álvaro Cunqueiro, who defined Galicia as the one of the thousand rivers. I probably also agree with you that it's short, because I probably have more. But you're looking at this law that this wealth, that identity sign of our Galicia, is more protected. And one of the best ways is to involve, of course, youth. There's no amnesty, no amnesty. I know that sometimes I don't know if they don't understand it or what they're looking for is that we get into that provocation, but since the accusations are very serious, I think my responsibility is also to clarify it. The forecasts that they proposed in the new article are already included in Article 12, which I find to be of important content. But it's just that, in addition, you know perfectly well the reports that the General Legal Counsel pointed out in December 2019. They also know the principle of proportionality in sanctions, they know perfectly well that there are certain facts that were already collected in other normative instruments. I think all of this knows him, because I want to think that they really form themselves, and I want to think that they read texts with constructive attitude -- and I say "I want to think" and I'm meant to make an effort, because really, I insist, today's debate, of course, was kinder than on other occasions --. In this first intervention, thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u34	Thank you very much, Mr. President. Good day again, gentlemen. The Galician Territory Management Act was entered into the Parliamentary Register on 24 September 2020. And its admission to the proceedings was carried out by the Bureau of Parliament at its meeting on 29 September, opening the deadline for the submission of amendments which ended on 19 October and 2 November 2020 for amendments to the whole and the articulated, respectively. In particular, the amendments to the full refund submitted by the parliamentary groups of the Galician Nationalist Bloc and the Galician Socialists were rejected at the plenary meeting on 10 November 2020. For its part, the amendments to the articulation presented and admitted to the procedure were a total of 306 -- 146 of the Parliamentary Group of Socialists of Galicia, 134 of the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block Galego and 26 of the People's Parliamentary Group of Galicia --. Commission 2nd, Territorial Planning, Public Works, Environment and Services, at its meeting on 13 November, appointed the presentation to prepare the report, which was formed on the same date. And at the following four meetings, the rapporteur and the rapporteurs -- Mr Martín Fernández Prado, by the Galician People's Parliamentary Group; Ms Alexandra Fernández Gómez, by the Galego Nationalist Block Parliamentary Group; and Mr Luis Manuel Álvarez Martínez, by the Galician Socialist Group -- they prepared the report with their final recommendations, which they issued on 30 November. Subsequently, the 2nd Committee on Territorial Planning, Public Works, Environment and Services, at its meeting on 11 December, approved the dictation I am presenting. It is therefore necessary to complete today the processing of this legislative procedure with the approval, if any, of the draft law on the management of Galician territory. And taking advantage of the Christmas spirit, I wish his lords good parties. Thank you very much.

I would say, Mr President, that, not by the Christmas spirit, but because I personally consider it, firstly, I would like to have a special thanks to the former Member of this Parliament, Mr Martín Fernández Prado, who was rapporteur in this Land Management Act. I think it's a person who, within his dialectics, permanently avoids clichés, which is something to thank in every parliamentary environment, and which should be something to thank socially in general. He has facilitated the work of writing this legislative text. I wish you the best in your new position as president of the Port Authority of Coruña. I also believe that it will be good for the Harbour Authority and I believe that this Parliament loses a good parliamentarian. When the debate on this entire Territorial Management Bill was held in the middle of November 10, since our parliamentary group said that the reasons and the need for a new law were not sufficiently accredited, because the mere fact -- it referred to this as well in the previous debate -- that this law has more than two decades of vigency is not sufficient, because there are many other earlier autonomous laws that do not need to be replaced. So argumentation is the same as at that time. Similarly, considering that land management policies are the basis for building a country, we see how since 2009 Galicia has progressively forgotten the idea of making a country; it has been forgetting the idea of having a pact for non-partisan territory and able to lay the foundations for medium-term development. The Land Management Guidelines, although it is true that they lost some of the strengths of the initial documents, still today they present a starting document that is much more attractive than the laws that developed later, where the liberal view of the soil becomes more present. By way of example, the failure of the 2016 Land Law was demonstrated by the shortage of new planning processes in our autonomous community. But the changes made in the existing urban regulations do not justify a new law on the territory, because that should precisely go contrary. Similarly, the theoretical need for new objectives to be addressed should, in any event, be preceded by an analysis of the results of the current Galician Land Management Act, both in terms of its degree of development and application as well as in terms of the vigency or capacity of the land management instruments it contains, which is not addressed either. And, at the same time, we also have to argue what those issues would be that cannot be addressed within the framework of the current legislation. As a clarification that the area of territorial management, and in particular urbanism, is situated in a permanent definition of its orientation with the governments of the People's Party in Galicia, we have the debate that will follow on the so-called Administrative Simplification Act and support for the economic reactivation of Galicia, which also includes urban aspects. Since there is no such prior analysis of the current law that the Galician Union avoids carrying out, it is inconsistent to motivate enough justification for its modification, and more so when, in fact, most of the aspects marked in the bill's explanatory statement are included in the approval, in 2011, of the Territory's own Management Guidelines. So much so that even the Galician Union has recognized that the territorial development model collected in the current Land Management Act -- which will be repealed today -- and in the 2011 Land Management Guidelines is flexible and has a journey; or when it highlights the usefulness of certain existing management instruments that are now intended to be eliminated. It is therefore surprising that the Council of Galicia now argues on the futility of certain instruments which contain the current Land Management Act and opts for its derogation, when it was precisely the self-government itself who disagreed to promote them, showing its own ineffectiveness, which now tries to transmutate in obsolescence of the previous text. It is a bill that, on the contrary, covers important and concrete changes that are needed by the legal body in order to be brought into practice, such as the problematic introduction of new figures such as those that consist of the so-called self-interest projects; instruments that are formulated in a way different from those of the current plans and projects of supra-municipal incidence, setting up as direct management tools by the Galician Union, which is able to harm the principle of municipal autonomy and which poses a threat to the entire territory, regardless of the soil rating, even for those with special protection. At the same time, this is a finding of the lack of guidance and lack of public management demonstrated in recent years for strategic planning and land management by the self-government, when it now promotes an option that distorts flexibility with liberalization and transition, and when in the current urban or industrial legislation there are figures to come to enable certain projects to be implemented. As always, the bill does not introduce any kind of real commitment or future obligation on the part of the Government of Galician Union. Our parliamentary group legitimately aspired to improve this unnecessary text at the stage of the presentation; an aspiration which was truncated by the People's Group, transferring the will of the Government of Galicia. Of the 134 amendments that we tabled with the best intention of improving the text from the Socialist Parliamentary Group, 17 were accepted. In fact, 11 were accepted because 6 coincided with other People's Group that referred to nothing less than the existence of the Territory's Institute of Studies, which disappeared from the initial text. We were able to trade four amendments, and the rest, that is, 113 were rejected. At this point, we must look at the rejected proposals, because the above figures could be reduced to statistical weaknesses. In the allotted time, I will try to make a few strokes about the amendments that have been registered, because speaking of the 113 rejected would be impossible in the time we have allocated, and that debate was also held in the press. We planned in our amendments, with regard to the guiding principles and criteria of the law itself, that the instruments provided for in the law were complementary and not exclusive to those which with regard to urban management are governed by their specific legislation. It was ultimately about providing legal security to an extremely complex area from a regulatory point of view, such as urbanism, because we all know that it is in the legal sphere where the real complexity is, in the orientation and interpretation of the rules. On territorial rationality, we thought that, with regard to certain existing developments, if those that were compatible were kept, because for those who are developing, which are not compatible with the territorial model that you want, it would require an adequacy rather than perpetuation of developing projects that could or should be corrected. On administrative competence, we thought that public urban activity was generally in the hands of municipalities, which would exercise many competences in urban matters which were expressly assigned to them or those which were not assigned to other administrations. Obviously, we all know that councils should respect -- like? -- legislation in the elaboration of their plans. They are subject to their approval, but they are directly responsible for this activity. By the way, one might wonder about the operation of these municipal certification bodies present in our urban regulations. With regard to citizen participation, we wanted any of the territorial management instruments covered by this law to be subject to a period of not less than 20 days of citizen participation through public exposure and hearing to local entities, prior to their approval. We believe, frankly, that in a matter of so much relevance and impact on citizenship as territorial management, it seems essential to ensure its participation; the same as when we ask that the process of participation be implemented through a specific plan that should be made public. With regard to the content of the Territorial Management Guidelines, which are also regulated, we wanted them to include a quantification of needs in different areas or areas of the territory and the setting of parameters that should be taken into account by urban management instruments, in order to ensure the availability of soil needed to achieve their purposes. Let us ask ourselves today how it is possible to refuse that there is a quantified analysis of the objective requirement for the availability of the needed soil. With regard to parliamentary control, the text to be adopted provides that Parliament shall be informed every three years. We understand that it is an excessive period and that it could be reported, without great complexity, annually, because what is too much for the Galician Parliament to know on an annual basis the degree of development of the objectives and the determinations of the territorial management guidelines? Is there already no system of indicators that can be modified or completed for monitoring and evaluation of those objectives and determinations? We also want Parliament to be informed of the progress, criteria and guidelines that should have been approved before by this House, with regard to parliamentary control, as did the previous one. We intend to set up the Management Board of the Territory of Galicia; a collective body of a representative or consultative type, with a composition, to ensure an institutional presence both of the departments of the Autonomous Community Administration and of the rest of the public administrations with competence in the territory, but also with representation of the most representative organizations of local, business and trade unions, and with active participation of associations and other entities related to the management of the territory, as well as the inclusion of experts and specialists in this area. We also wanted, within the content of the integrated territorial plans, to include a weighted estimate of the soil surfaces that should be reserved for some of the following purposes: the construction of official protective dwellings, both public and private, or the public promotion of industrial soil. It is appropriate to know which part of these purposes is not shared when planning and designing the content of integrated territorial plans is required. We also want these integrated territorial plans to carry out an economic study on the coherence between the proposed rules and actions and the availability of resources. Would it not be appropriate for such an analysis to connect proposals that are supposed to be based on needs with available resources? Because this amendment was not accepted either. We propose, with regard to the effectiveness of the territorial plans, that when those plans had an impact on existing urban plans, the agreement and the final approval of those plans necessarily needed the specific points where the plans could be modified, without prejudice, of course, to agree on appropriate procedures in the municipalities to adapt them to their determinations. With regard to the management of projects of self-interest, we intend that, in the event of the exercise of expropriation in favour of a private person, the beneficiary should justify the need to articulate such a process of forced expropriation in order of a manifest public interest, as well as the impossibility of being able to access other systems provided for his enjoyment or acquisition. It would ultimately be a matter of limiting the use of expropriatory power in an absolutely guaranteed way, which, although we agree that it can be understood that already exists in the regulations, would add additional protection to the owners. We also want the role of coordinated action programmes to be created, so that a multi-annual plan relating to the whole of the Community or to some of its sectoral or cross-sectoral areas is drawn up, incorporating the actions proposed by the various public administrations or bodies operating within the autonomous Community. To conclude, I mean, with regard to the procedure for the approval of the land management instruments to be subject to the ordinary strategic assessment, which we believe is necessary for the Galician Union to communicate to this Parliament quarterly the approval of the remaining land management instruments that this law collects, with the indication of its main characteristics. Again, ladies and gentlemen, it would be necessary to inform the Galician Parliament of the development of these actions. It's a problem, we believe, of will and of impulse to transparency; it's not about access to information, but about ease of access to that information. Finally, we would like to point out that with regard to the designation of the Territorial Management Guidelines, the Council of Galicia, within a maximum of three months from the date of entry into force of this law, a document containing the new classification of the determinations of the existing Land Management Guidelines has been approved, because, as I said at the beginning of my speech, the Council of Galicia always regulates for others, but it never imposes its own obligations and much less specific deadlines to develop the necessary instruments and determinations. So we are going to oppose this legal text for the reasons we already set out at the plenary session on 10 November, but in addition, because we were unable to substantially modify the law in terms we considered necessary. Nothing more and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u42	Thank you, President. In Galicia we have an important problem, a very big problem, with territorial planning. And the main cause of this problem is that until now no space planning has been developed that has ordered the territory from an integral perspective. There is neither the country scale nor the sub-regional scale; we only have land management guidelines that work as general recommendations, but without the put into operation after integrated territorial plans that implement them in a specific regulation. We have no instruments that really serve to govern the territory. The only effective planning in our territory is that of the general municipal management plans that are centered inside the municipal term. But the problem is, for many years now, urban processes have overflowed this milestone. Forty years ago, it began a process of widespread urbanization on the Atlantic axis, or that the forms of urban life were coexisted in land-based settlements and that social relationships, types of displacement, distances, relationships between residential space and economic activities, land-uses. And all of this, all of these transformations in recent decades, have made the territorial dynamics throughout Galicia move ever more towards an articulation on a wider scale, on a sub-regional or supra-municipal scale -- they call it what they want -- It is that our territorial reality basically translates into metropolitan and comarcas fields. What is clear is that since the sum of municipal planning it is impossible to articulate Galician territorial reality today, and much less considering the complexity of the urban system and the polycentric model, as is ours. And my question is, why don't we have territorial planning on that scale in Galicia? Why do other autonomous communities have it? Why do Euskadi, Catalonia or Andalusia have sub-regional planning for their territory? We don't have it for the sake of this government's will, and it took us until the year 95 to really assume the competences of land management. We're late; we're late with the first land management law in the 95th. But at least he set the first framework that finally made it possible for us to have territorial planning on that scale. They don't want to plan, because planning means setting limits, and that's why the legal text they're going to pass today is not a new law. You just want to assume the competences that facilitate deregulation, the ballast, the business, but not the ones that really serve to order the territory. And the second problem that we have -- which is also serious, it's heavy and what we have to answer -- is the dynamic of extensive urbanization over the last few decades, which is the result of a logic of fast-duty extraction and which has been fuelled by a framework of policy where there are no limits and there are no criteria that are the result of a public assessment -- public assessment and not only private interests -- of an assessment of the costs and benefits of medium- and long-term interventions. Without planning, Galicia's territorial dynamics at the moment is subject to the only criterion of economic profitability, and thus priority is given to using new soil because it is more profitable in the economy than to face transformation and improvement of existing ones. And even public administrations themselves assume this logic of immediate profitability. For example, when deciding on the location of supramunicipal equipment, they are based on concentration and cheap soil criteria, but not on supramunicipal planning criteria. And, of course, what are the consequences of this? What are the mobility costs after? What are the environmental costs?, the cost of a model where the territory is fragmented? And the same is true of economic activities, which, in most cases, do not meet the criteria of territorial coherence, but rather deepen the model of reducing initial costs without assessing the economic viability of interventions; without assessing, for example, what is the cost of infrastructure that should be added to these interventions, because sometimes the cheap, at first, ends up very expensive. And without supra-municipal planning, the result is an urbanized territory that lacks urban quality, with inadequate densities and with tissue disconnected from each other and disconnected from existing tissues. And without supra-municipal planning over all these decades, urbanization of all this time has not served to provide, to improve, not to give greater social cohesion, nor to improve mobility patterns, nor to guarantee access to housing; for the only one who served was to rapidly extract surpluses at the cost of territorial fragmentation. That's why we're not only late in planning, not only forty years late in planning, but now we have to give more powerful and more complex answers to intervene on this territorial reality. Therefore, the fundamental and priority objectives for a new law that really assumes or that will take on territorial planning competences should be, at least, developing sub-regional planning -- it's urgent. Secondly, planning which is considered to be public interest and which is therefore not subject solely to major economic interests, but rather to public, social and collective objective criteria. And thirdly, include criteria for the sustainability and resilience of territorial values. In other words, the costs are assessed in the medium and long term. But this law, this new law, does not respond to any of this; on the contrary, it favors that any timely intervention of soil transformation does not need to be contemplated or connected with any territorial planning instrument, nor even with the only ones that were vigilant, which are municipal ones. You may be disconnected from any schedule. They are necessary, and they must be guided by the principle of environmental, social and economic resilience, in the short, medium and long term. A less vulnerable territorial model is the strength of the future, and we are seeing it now, that we are at a time of great transformation and that we have great vulnerabilities in the territory that conditions us. They are used to order infrastructure, housing, extractive activities, energy exploitation, issues that would deserve different treatment and different criteria and standards. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u73	The People's Parliamentary Group in Galicia has the floor of Mr Tellado Filgueira.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u74	Thank you, President. We have tabled the proposal for an administrative simplification law and support for Galicia's economic reactivation, a document which was jointly worked out by the People's Parliamentary Group in collaboration with the Government of Galicia. We start from the obvious consideration that we are at a tremendously exceptional moment that requires all the effort and commitment of citizens, but also all the effort and commitment of public administrations. The administration must be a support for the activities of professionals and companies, never a brake, especially at the most complex times, like these we are living in. It is necessary, therefore, for us to change administrative culture, and this bill is an opportunity to do so. The reactivation is, of course, due to the fact that governments promote activity and facilitate administrative procedures so that businesses and professionals can develop their economic activity, to attract new investments to Galicia and to support initiatives by both entrepreneurs and already consolidated projects. This is what the Galician Union has been doing since the beginning of this pandemic with this plan for economic reactivation, which has enabled investments of more than EUR 3,000 million to be mobilized, with the reinforcement of public services, the reactivation of the economy and the protection of employment as fundamental objectives, and also with the Plan for the Rescue of self-employed and microenterprises, which has enabled aid to be developed for a value of EUR 84 million for direct support to the hospitality and self-government. The road map of the Government of Galicia and the Galician People's Party for Economic Reactivation continues with this bill that we present today for its consideration and subsequent initiation of the parliamentary process. The Popular Group presents this bill that has a very clear objective: to generate trust. Trust is fundamental to achieving the reactivation of our economy, to encourage consumption and also to launch a job protection message. To achieve this trust, this law works on three fundamental axes. The first is the axillization of administrative procedures; the second is support and funding directed at companies and professionals; and the third is anticipation, anticipation to take advantage of the opportunity represented by European funds. Agitating administration is probably the main axis of this rule. This law aims to speed up administrative procedures to boost economic activity and the creation of new projects in Galicia. How do you do it? Simplifying bureaucratic procedures, eliminating duplication, saving costs, reducing processing times, simultaneous procedures and reports and encouraging coordination between different administrations. To emphasize that this axillization in no case means reducing guarantees or eliminating protections to other fundamental values, such as environmental, cultural or natural values. The second axis of this law seeks to support business initiatives, especially the younger ones and especially those located on rural borders. It designs financing mechanisms for small businesses, supporting their implementation, it creates a multiple support system for professional companies -- that investment care system, the Office of Doing Business -- and the individualized accompaniment system for large projects. It also provides tax benefits to support rural areas and regulates, for the first time, the concept of tractor projects, in addition to redefining strategic industrial projects and priority business initiatives, which broaden their capacity to absorb more initiatives and more projects. This law anticipates the arrival of European funds, European funds which undoubtedly represent a unique opportunity to minimize the impact of the pandemic and to support companies and professionals to transform our economy, promoting a more sustainable, greener, more digital model. But to take this opportunity, we must be prepared, and therefore anticipation is the third axis of this law. To create a complete mechanism, to create a comprehensive mechanism for planning, programming, management and control of European funds, strengthening the Interdepartmental Commission Next Generation, establishing a technical committee to assist the commission, establishing working groups that will work to simplify the procedures for grants and contracts, homogenizing models and good practices. On the other hand, the law provides for tax benefits for certain business initiatives. I want to highlight several. Deduction of 100 % in costly heritage transmissions, in tax on documented heritage transmissions and legal acts, for the acquisition of real estate found in unpopulated or rural areas; this deduction will be 50 % in the rest of the territory of our community. Deduction of 100 % of the tax burden on documented legal acts, notary documents on capital transmission tax and documented legal acts, for acquisition, grouping, division, segregation, declaration of new work and horizontal division of immovable property in small areas or rural areas; also, the deduction will be 50 % in the rest of our community's territory. The rule also provides for the creation of a series of catalogues by sectors of activities which will collect all administrative processes that can be required for the implementation of new projects, as well as a series of forms, applications templates and documentation associated with the above procedures, which will without any doubt mean a great help and simplification for companies. The standard also includes the creation of the SAI (Investment Care System) for electronic access to all information and documentation specified and for real-time processing status of all such files. Creation of the Office Doing Bussines Galicia as a unit specializing in the accompaniment and processing of these industrial projects characterized as strategic. And in addition, this law proposes the development of an individualized accompaniment system with a network of business support offices. Article 56 regulates the concept of tractor project, of which we have seen talking a lot without being perfectly regulated. These are strategic industrial initiatives that affect various economic sectors to generate growth, employment and competitiveness, acting as a vertebrate of other business initiatives and meeting the requirements for funding by European funds. Establishment of specific financing instruments for e-startup initiatives, entrepreneurs and other small businesses. As I told you, this law will strengthen the Next Generation Commission, as regulated in Article 60, as a planning body, direction and coordination of business initiatives that can be financed from these funds. The technical committee, as regulated in Article 61, will also advise on the evaluation of plans and projects that are examined by the European Commission. And it's all planned and designed so that the day after the adoption of this standard from the Galician Union, we can set up working groups, which will operate immediately in the simplification of subsidy and recruitment procedures, homogenizing models and homogenizing good practices. Finally, this proposal also seeks to encourage participation -- in particular, it is collected in Article 63 -- to provide for three instruments in fund planning. The first, already known, the commission of economic experts, who has been working in our community since last May. The second, the tables of social dialogue, which have been in operation for quite a while. And finally, a working group with the Galega Federation of Municipalities and Provinces. That is, this law counts with the main institutions, with social and economic agents, with representatives of workers and businesses and with municipalities, a broad representation of the Greek society. I'm done. In short, this is a law that continues the work that the Government of Greece started last March, supporting professionals and businesses, reactivating the economy and consolidating tax reductions. A law that bets on a new administrative culture, eliminates obstacles and drives new business projects in Galicia. A law that lowers taxes, creates mechanisms for consulting and accompaniment to companies and regulates the shape of tractor projects indispensable to the definition of Galicia's future. A law that anticipates the arrival of European funds, developing planning, management and control instruments, to take advantage of the opportunity of these funds to transform Galicia's economy into a greener economy, into a more digital economy. A law designed by and for professionals and companies in Greece, which we hope to have the support of the political forces that make up this chamber to initiate its parliamentary process. I come forward with our will, the will of the proposed group, to count on all, to enrich this text among all and to make a law with which this parliament feels fully identified and a law that serves to deal with this triple pandemic: health, economic and social, and that among all, together, we may be able to convert this crisis into an opportunity, the opportunity that we need for the Galician society to move forward in administrative simplification and to advance in the economic reactivation that we all want. Nothing more and thank you very much.

The climate emergency statements began in the summer of 2019 as symbolic statements, which joined echo organizations and a good number of groups, including universities and other youth organizations, such as the young Swedish activist Greta Thunberg. Subsequently, the idea was silent and part of the institutions, ranging from the European Parliament to the governments of the different countries and the autonomous and local governments, which were adopted at institutional level, but modified the texts of the declaration originally proposed by those organizations. In September 2019, the Congress of Deputies adopted the Declaration of the State of Climate Emergency in Spain; a declaration which did not bring any concrete action, but established a framework in which climate change was placed as a priority of the country's policies in a cross-cutting manner. This type of declaration was evolving, and so, in January 2020, the Council of Ministers approved the Declaration Agreement on Climate and Environmental Emergency in Spain, which is not the same as declaring the state of climate emergency in Galicia, as its motion calls for. It also approved the 2019-2023 integrated Regional Energy and Climate Plan, the 1st Strategic Development Plan which includes 170 measures covering all Galician departments with climate change-related competences. [[Applause]] So today we are proposing six questions which we believe should go forward without any problems. The first is that we need a consensus in this country. They have to abandon from the German Government the strategy of unilaterality and they have to replace the current Law of Wind Use, instead of amending the law which has just been introduced into Parliament, by a process of consensus which harmonises the objectives of industrial development, but also the necessary environmental custody and also the necessary return to the territory and society. We demand that a new, participatory, wind sector plan be made, because we cannot continue to base the plan we have, based on the current map in a reality that is 1997. It is necessary to take a look at it to ensure transparency, environmental assessment and guarantees, and also that expropriation processes are limited to a minimum. We also wagered by strengthening the staff of the Galician administration, by providing the Inega with a relevant role, and by creating secure measures for them and the owners, such as that demand of the Galego Wind Observatory to arbitrate a record in which all contracts, prices, and that this allows each small owner to have information within his reach in negotiating with these large companies. In this sense, we understand from the BNG that most of the existing problems, that most of the concern in the territory and the distortions, would be resolved if there really was a development strategy that put collective interests at the center and linked that to the wind strategy. In that sense, we insist on the question of public participation, not only of the Galician Union, but also of the Greek municipalities, but also of the experience of formulae such as energy communities and so on. We would also like to introduce that proposal from the Galego Wind Observatory of a model contract to allow individuals and mountain communities to have instruments for negotiation, to resume this need for industrial development and environmental restoration plans in the areas where parks are located, and that this also has guarantees of development for the areas. And it's also a pending question, which is linking all of this to the innovative German fabric. Fourthly, another of the proposals we put on the table is that we correct the deficiencies that exist today in that wind canon arbitrated by the Galician Union, and in the Environmental Compensation Fund. We've been debating this a lot of times. The Fund requires transparency and that it mostly goes to the most affected places, and the canon is an absolute nonsense and injustice that nowadays continues to compete for mills, instead of power, when there are so many repots. In that sense, I want to recall here the estimate from the Wind Observatory that local and rural incomes for landowners only think about about about 5% of the actual, absolutely multi-millionaire billing that these companies have; therefore, a very limited return that needs to be corrected. And fifthly, because we are asking for increased monitoring of environmental guarantees, and for that we need to develop management figures, we need to develop figures like the expansion of the Natura Network, and we need to support industrial development to environmental well-being, and not go in the opposite line, as proved today morning, which will basically put the nature network's master plan to the service of what the wind bosses order. And finally, we introduced a question that also seems to us to be no smaller. We're at a time of many changes in the energy aspect, we're at a time when we have in our country of comarcas that have been through these years with an installed thermal that's going to close, and what's not from receipt is that there's going to be a handful of renewables announced and published the order last December 4 by the Central Government in which there are two major absences. On the one hand, we do not incorporate criteria that are not exclusively economical when we arbitrate those pockets, and on the other hand, we insist that there should be specific thrusts of renewables for the areas affected by these closures. This is a possibility that does not invent the Galego Nationalist Block, this is a possibility that contemplates the legal framework for a transition just to the brink of the strategic framework of energy and climate, and therefore the logical thing is that, if Endesa leaves The Bridges with her ass in the air, at least there are a few guarantees that there will be a specific thrust for this part of the territory, so that she cannot be the same company that plunders a part of our country because she obtains benefits through this new pox, and that at least in the way of wind development, which will irremediably affect these regions, there is a specific weight and there can also be a participation by these localities and these districts. The opinion of Mrs. Counsellor for Employment and Equality, on his own request, to report on the evolution of the health emergency of the covid-19 in relation to the general forecasts and lines of action of his department
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u167	To make this appearance, you have the word of the Employment and Equality Counsellor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2020-12-22-DSPG018.u168	Good afternoon. Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I am indeed present in this House and on my own request to inform, on the one hand, of the strategic lines of the Employment and Equality Council, but also to take account of the evolution of the health and economic emergency of the Covid-19 in the field of the competences of this board. This council is a new creation council, and it's a council that shows the commitment of the Greek Government to the two areas that refer to its name: employment and equality. In the first case, because we give the protagonists of an area which is vitally important to the economic and social recovery of the community in the coming years, and in the second case, because we rise to the advice of a general secretariat, that of Equality, precisely by launching a very clear message; the message of which we will promote equality, but labour equality and co-responsibility to achieve not only the economic and social recovery of Galicia, but also because we think that equality in the labour market, that equality in education and that equality in education are the fundamental basis for combating gender violence. As you know, we found ourselves in a historic moment, in a moment of an unprecedented global crisis of great impact on our social fabric, on our business fabric and on our work and industrial fabric of which we still do not, unfortunately, know the concrete date of its end, its output and the real dimensions and repercussions that it will have. In view of all this, the Department of Employment and Equality of the Galician Union is planning to develop -- and it is already developing -- actions oriented, on the one hand, to the protection and promotion of employment in our community, but also to guarantee the principle of equality. So I'm going to structure the appearance at two points: the first relative to the lines of action that the Council had in relation to the crisis of the coward to the moment, and a second already in relation to the strategic lines for the next year 2021. From the point of view of the measures articulated, in this case in the field of equality for the shelter or for the protection of victims of gender-based violence against the coward in 2021, in particular we have articulated two types of means: on the one hand, those relating to the services provided to the victims of violence and on the other hand, to the reference to economic support. With regard to services, the Council of Galicia Council of 20 March has already approved a plan to strengthen the care of victims of gender-based violence; a plan with 17 basic means which, on the one hand, sought to maintain with full intensity the existing services for the victims, but also to strengthen and create other new ones that we considered necessary. So we have kept operational in any case, and we have adapted them, in their case, to telephone services, on-line care and legal advice, in this case, on-line; psychological support services, which we have maintained in presence, but also through telephone sessions and telephone follow-up; we have maintained services of an administrative nature also on-line, so that all economic aid could be processed at the electronic headquarters of the Union; and we have maintained the point of coordination of protection orders. And the Integral Women Recovery Centre has maintained permanent contact with all users. As I told you, we also implemented new user services. In this way, reception centres have been implemented or increased, providing for a possible increase in demand, with alternative resources such as hostels, hostels and pensions. In this sense, it is also worth noting the work carried out by the CIMs, the information centres for women -- the 82 CIMs that provide their services in collaboration between municipalities and the autonomous community that finances them in part -- which, far from closing their doors, increased their hours from 8 p.m. to 9 p.m., thus ensuring ongoing face-to-face attention. We count on the collaboration of the Galician Taxi Federation to ensure the service of the transfer of victims to these reception centres, and we extend the Social Rent Bonus to the victims of gender violence, assuming 100 percent of the rent. As I told them, support was not only about services, but also about economic aid to victims. So during these months of the year we already have 639 beneficiaries of monthly payments of regular aid to victims of gender-based violence and 341 beneficiaries of annual single payment aid to victims of violence with special employment difficulties. And we also grant compensation for the offender's insolvency, in this case to eleven beneficiaries in the year. This year 2020 we continue with two economic aids launched in 2019 which are pioneers in the Spanish State. The first is aid for children under 30 years of age who are victims of gender-based violence -- two beneficiaries from year to year -- and the second is economic aid or compensation for women who have been seriously injured and who have required hospitalization -- three would benefit -- All of this was accompanied by a wide-ranging institutional dissemination campaign, reminding women of the telephones and the aids, support and resources at their disposal. As I said earlier, the impact of the crisis has also affected very significantly the work fabric of the Greek language. And in this context, it is also of utmost importance that work has been carried out since the Employment Council in its position as labour authority since the Directorate-General for Labour Relations. This general address was able to process, within less than five days since the entry into force, 4000 ERTEs in the second wave and made it possible in the same way and with the table agility and speed the disaffectation of the companies and the corresponding workers affected to these files for the return to activity. I'd like to take this opportunity to speak not only about this general address, but about all the Council employees and make a public thank-you to all of them, because without each of them's work in the processing of these files and all of those I'm going to account for right now it would be impossible if they had carried out the performances and activities and had gone ahead with all the programs I'm going to talk about here. Returning to the Directorate-General for Labour Relations, he was also able to maintain a unified approach in the resolution of these ERTE files throughout the autonomous community. This was achieved through continuous interpretations and information notes on how the ERTs would have to be processed as the different state rules regulating them have been adopted. In this way, we kept in touch with the professional schools of lawyers, managers, social graduates' schools, to ensure proper processing and, as I say, a unique and coherent processing in the four territorial heads of the Council. The Issga, the Institute of Occupational Safety and Health, also performed a fundamental work during the pandemic, during the covid-19, in this case in prevention and safety and occupational health. Issga, as you know, has two fundamental functions: the first is to accompany companies in the development of prevention and safety and occupational health plans, but it also has the role of helping them in the consultations they make to the institute with criteria of maximum technicality and a high degree of specialization, both in the public and in the private sphere. In this context, in the first phase of the pandemic, he attended nearly 800 technical consultations, was able to prepare 76 documents between guides and preventive recommendations and gave advice and technical assistance to 100 percent of the demands made from different sectors, and they are to highlight the automotive, construction, naval and field or forest sector. It was also made available to all board members of the Board, drawing up specific action protocols for re-integration. Issga has become, by empowering Royal Decree Law 21/2020, empowered authority within the framework of prevention and hygiene, and in this sense twelve people -- Issga's technical staff -- have performed 28 actions on the basis of this ability basically ensuring the availability of protective equipment for workers, monitoring ventilation measures, disinfection and effective reorganization of working times and spaces to return to work. Issga's work is constant and will adapt to different developments; it's also reviewing and updating its documents according to the scenarios that derive from the evolution of the pandemic. And so, in this second wave, he was able to approve, elaborate and disseminate to the self-employed and micro-enterprises a brochure and a guide called Keys to the Prevention of Covid-19 in your company to respond to the major doubts of companies in the face of possible contagion in a workplace. It also carried out technical advice for the preparation of reports on hygienic masks; it issued a report to the People's Valedora on preventive measures to protect the health of workers from home support services during the pandemic; it carried out technical advice to the College of Journalists of Galicia to minimize the risk of infection of professionals in communication; and it also conducted training against the curfew-19 to the educational community at the request of the Council of Culture, Education and University. Finally, we must highlight the creation in the last quarter of the year of a covid-19 reference unit within the Issga to provide -- now in 2021 -- constant support in the work environment and it will have EUR 30,000. We also put in place adaptation measures in the field of training and placement, primarily linked to the flexibility and adaptation of courses, so that it was easier to move from presences to being given via teleformation or virtual classes. Since March 15th, all the training provided by the Centre for New Technologies in Galicia has been carried out in distance modes, and still with the pandemic we have been able to take forward 19 training actions and 390 pupils, and even now there are 48 students in three training actions. We also strengthen the cleaning of both employment offices and training centres, investing more than 2 million euros. But in this context it was also necessary to support economically our small businesses, our autonomous ones, and that is why it was key to the work of the Directorate-General for Employment, Self-employment and Social Economy, which led and launched the rescue plan for self-employed people and micro-enterprises. I can tell you that this plan now has 39,600 applications. We have invested more than eleven million euros, and since 2009, over nine hundred women's projects have been funded and over six hundred jobs have been created. In this area of promoting equality, we will continue to bet -- and, if necessary, more -- on the conciliation and co-responsibility programmes, on the conciliation programmes in collaboration with local authorities, but also on the conciliation and co-responsibility programmes that we have been working on since the Galician Union, rising, in this case, the calls, by which we finance the reduction in the number of single-parent men and families by 41% over 2020. But, as I said before, full equality not only can we achieve with the fight against gender-based violence, not only can we achieve with cross-cutting actions to promote equality, but we must also achieve it with genuine labour equality. In 2021, they will make more precise than ever the support they promote and ensure that women can work on equal conditions with men, that they have equal access to work and that they also ensure their job maintenance by supporting, of course, the assumptions of conciliation and co-responsibility. In the field of equality of work, as a novelty, we will train the companies themselves, in addition to the workers, in the field of equality, to be able to shut down in the productive fabric as regards labour equality. Technology tools will be created to facilitate the implementation of equality plans. Aid will be stepped up both for the development and for the follow-up of equality plans in companies. Since then, we have met on eight occasions with the agents present at the social dialogue tables to deal with topics such as equal counselling, the execution of educational content and equal tools, the Empreganav project, the Next Generation funds, or the Act of Reactivation.
