It shows the agreements made within the social dialogue is, as I told you, for example, the Empreganav project, a project that counts with an investment of EUR 3.3 million, thanks to European funds, co-financed also by the Galician Union, and which aims to reintegrate unemployed people from the auxiliary shipbuilding industry in Galicia. It is a pioneering project that aims to guide, shape and retrain; ultimately, to encourage both self-employment and to support the relocation of these workers. It will be developed in 2021 and in the first half of the year 2022, and we have just signed, with representatives of both Asime and Workers' and UGT Committees, the agreement to start the initial phase of the project. The Issga -- Institute of Occupational Safety and Health -- is going to continue to comply with its safety and health policy at work, and will continue, on the one hand, supporting the pandemic with that covered reference unit I just mentioned, which will try to give an axile response and an effective response to the prevention of the risk of transmission by the coward, but on the other hand, it will continue its work. On the one hand, agreements with social agents on safety and health strategies at work, which ended in 2020, are being extended. And this will be done with the firm intention of setting up a working group that is able to debate about the link between public health and its relationship with work health, and ultimately with the intention of having a more comprehensive view in the new strategy on the prevention of occupational risks in the workplace, taking into account, precisely, the effects of covid. We're going to continue to work in the field of labor charity. Issga has set up a working group with social agents for the analysis and prevention of labour inequality in Galicia, and we will therefore continue in the annual planning of activities in Issga, focusing precisely on all those activities that, by the fruit of this work and this group, have shown that they have increased their sinality or have reiterated the accident in the corresponding sectors. Finally, Issga will continue with advocacy campaigns and awareness campaigns. We are going to focus on these campaigns in particular on risks arising from preventive measures in teleworking, the use of mobile devices and the psychosocial risks generated by the pandemic. It is the responsibility of public administrations to design new measures that adapt to changing reality, and especially in the current socio-economic context; these responses that have a lot to do with the training of our workers and the work orientation. That's why we bet on creating a new public employment service in Galicia. We are committed to making an integral renewal of the Public Service of Employment of the Galician community, a commitment that goes from then on to modernizing the infrastructure itself -- and in this sense we will complete the work of Lugo's office -- as we have also approved the projects to bid for the works of Coruña, Cangas, Ponteareas and Verín. But what's so important is that we're going to make an absolute change in the Public Employment Service system. It is about setting up a new operating system for the management and control of demand for employment, employment and training. It is a project of about six million euros that aims, through artificial intelligence, on the one hand, to bring forward the needs of the real-time labour market and, on the other hand, to adapt itself also to job seekers, in order to improve the processing of data and information relating to these applicants, not only about their training and their location, but about their abilities, about their skills, or even about their job preferences. All of this will allow a real-time crossing -- as I tell you -- of demand and job offer. It will therefore allow more efficient labour orientation, based on market demand, and will allow what is so important, adapt training, adapt the training routes of our workers and our workers to these needs. In this line, and in line with the recommendation of different national and international bodies on the need to analyse and change the demand for skills and qualifications that will be faced with employment in the coming years, we have also commissioned a study of emerging technologies that will be carried out over the next year, which basically aims to analyze or implement fourteen emerging technologies in fifteen sectors. This study aims at the following: firstly, to analyze the current situation, to analyse the different economic sectors or the impact that emerging technologies can have on the future in the workplace; to identify the innovations that affect each sector and to identify the existing training deficiencies in the staff working for these sectors so that we can adapt jobs; and to conclude with a document where it proposes or develops the qualifications needed for each of these sectors and the training modules needed to develop the necessary training offer. In this 2021 since the Directorate-General for Training and Employment, we will maintain, in addition to this reform of the Public Employment Service, all of the training actions, both for the unemployed and for the unemployed. We will therefore maintain all training actions for unemployed workers who are given in accredited training centres, in this case, with a call of more than EUR 5 million this year for 2021 on 2020. It is EUR 40 million to fund 841 courses and will form a total of 10,549 people. We will maintain training actions in companies engaged in recruitment with a biannual call that will allow nearly 6,000 students to participate and will also maintain training actions in the vocational training centres themselves for employment, in particular, in our 12 centres will be more than 2,300 participants in a total of 160 courses. We will also maintain -- as I told you -- all the training for the employed, for the employed, in the Employment Training Plan directed at the employed, but also with the training units directed at the training of workers employed in strategic sectors; this year we benefit from this plan 1,735 students with 442 training actions. And of course, the recognition of professional skills acquired through work experience will be maintained as well as evidence of evaluation in key competences in order to be able to access the level 2 and 3 professional certificates. We intend to meet a call for 4,000 people. Employment is the ultimate goal and purpose of all training, placement and guidance activities. So, from the very first moment, we sit not only with social dialogue but with the Galician Self-Government Bureau to define the rescue plan for self-government and micro-enterprises -- of what I've already told them -- and that's why we're going to be able to carry out actions like this in the next year 2021. Thus, economic items for self-employed and micro-enterprises have increased in this budget by 2021 by 310 %. In this way, we will be able to renew the Rescue Plan for self-employed people and micro-enterprises by adapting ourselves in January to the needs, on the one hand, of health measures, and, on the other hand, also to the needs of the representatives of the workers, businesses and self-employed within the framework of social dialogue. In any case, what I can assure you is that they will be aided with consensus -- as in this case with this plan -- and immediate aid, axial aid, easy to process and charge immediately, as I tell them. But we're also going to launch new programs not only for job maintenance, but also for generating new jobs. First, we will launch a programme that will promote new business initiatives regardless of their legal form, whether they are autonomous or micro-enterprises. And the only requirement is that they meet needs not covered in the territory or that they satisfy value chains generated by other industries or services present in the territory. We will continue with the Autonomous Bonus, with EUR 6 million for next year and we will also continue to support the reconciliation of the autonomous ones, the generational relief, the second opportunity and the mentoring programme, the tutoring program for self-employed people, which is allowing themselves to consolidate and help autonomous people to reorient their business. By next year we will lay the foundations for the social dialogue and the Self-Government Bureau of the Self-Employment Strategy for the year 2021 ahead and will also promote the virtual office of the current self-employment. But we also have to maintain support for people's recruitment, not only for enterprise or for the generation of new companies, and we will do so by continuing with programmes such as, for example, the Galicia Employment Programme, especially by supporting recruitment in women's businesses, but also by hiring in the ordinary business of people with disabilities and people at risk of social exclusion. We will also maintain aid for recruitment in municipalities and aid for recruitment by non-profit entities. I'm starting to realize the social economy side. The social economy is of great importance within the project of this council and the Council of Galicia and has already shown it since 2016 by Junta with the adoption of the Social Economy Act; a law that has made us the first autonomous community to be endowed with a law to regulate the sector. The social economy has a proven 19-21 strategy in Galicia, and the social economy has an increasingly consolidated presence in the territory, both in Galicia and the rest of Spain and in the European landscape. It has shown, on the one hand, that it is much more resistant to job destruction, it destroys around 6 % of jobs less than the rest of the organizations, and it currently has a weight of around 7% of GDP in Galicia. In Galicia this year we were able to build, still with the pandemic, 87 cooperative societies, 23 working societies, 4 special employment centres and 1 insertion company. We therefore exceed a total of more than 2,000 active entities between cooperatives, working societies, special employment centres and insertion companies. So in the year 2021, we'll continue to bet on the social economy, and we'll do it with programs and projects we'll talk about -- as it can't be otherwise -- at the Galego Social Economy Forum and at the Galician Social Economy Council, because, as I say, listening to the collectives, it's how we actually can promote public policies that are agreed and adjusted to the needs of each sector. The social economy increases in our budgets by 22%. We have 33 million intended solely for action for the year 2021; actions that we can organize in three major groups. The first are aimed at the creation and/or consolidation of social economy enterprises. Some are already known programs, such as the Aprol-Economy Social, which is intended to generate employment in cooperatives and in working societies, but also with three new programs: the Es-Transforma, which is intended to recover companies in another legal way that, for economic reasons or for lack of generational relief, have prospects of closure for becoming social economy entities; the Es-Factory, a second program, which is a programme of social economy accelerators which basically aims to guide and accompany the promoters of these business projects linked to business opportunities in the neighbouring economy; and the Good consolidation, which is a line of aid directed to existing social economy companies, but which have a minimum seniority and need some kind of renewal, adaptation, linked to the new economic context and also to the new digital age. A second group of actions, also very important, seeks to disseminate and raise awareness of the social economy, to get to know its values and to get to know the entities that make up it. Some of these actions will also be known as, for example, the Eusumo Network, for the promotion of cooperativeism and the social economy, but we will also launch a new programme, the Es-Next programme, which aims, precisely, to enhance the quality of products, the quality of services and the benefits that the social economy brings to our community in order to thus stimulate demand. And a third group of programmes, within the social economy, are programmes designed to promote inclusive employment, both for people with disabilities and for those at risk of exclusion. With these actions we hope to benefit 130 companies directly, giving work to more than 3,600 people. It is the aid to special employment centres, the aid to insertion companies, the programme that we have already launched for dual training for people with intellectual disabilities, which continues in 2021, the development for jobs aimed at people who perceive Risga and a new line of aid for financial rebalancing to special employment centres of social initiative and also to inclusion companies. Ladies and gentlemen, I think that what I have just explained is evident from the fact that the Galician Council and this Employment and Equality Council are working decisively, aware of our responsibility and also aware that there are many families, many companies, who are and live in times of great difficulty. I am fully aware that to achieve this goal the only way is to involve all social agents, all economic agents and all labor agents in our community. I therefore express my commitment to continue in the path of permanent social dialogue, consensus, to articulate measures, such as rescue plans to maintain and contain the effects caused by short-term cover, but also with new programs that allow flexible training adapted to the new digital context and the start-up of this new Employment Galego Service, equipped with tools that guarantee real, genuine interconnectivity and at the moment between supply and demand. These are actions that complement these new employment and social economy programmes that are close to what I have just spoken to you about. Ultimately, direct assistance, training, effective employment services that promote employability and which form the basis for sustainable development of our community, for a Galicia free of violence against men and women and for an equality Galicia. Nothing else. Thank you very much.

We think that with the investment that we are going to be able to carry out this year 2021 and also in 2022 -- and in part with European funds -- we will be able to implement and reform -- as I tell you -- employment and equality measures. We invest much more than the Bipartite -- they like it or not -- in the matter of equality, twice. Only from the General Secretariat of Equality have we invested 30 million euros. The budget goes up by 10%, to which all the conciliation programmes of the Social Policy Council must be added, as were you, since the former Vice-Presidentship of Equality and Welfare. So we went up twice as much of the budget on equal terms as during the Bipartite. We have risen by 46% of the conciliation actions and the co-responsibility actions. The co-responsibility and conciliation budget is not lower. Of the 25 million of the Directorate-General for Labour Relations, two million are to be added only to work equality. The Directorate General for Training and Placement is going to invest -- as I tell you -- 6 million euros in reforming the entire Public Employment Service. We grew by 300 % on self-government and micro-enterprises. We found ourselves, finally, a year before this council and, of course, the whole of Galicia -- like all governments in the world -- faced with very important challenges, but I also believe that we have a real strategic organization, with consolidated proposals and proposals that are budgeted and with genuine planning. 2021 presents itself as an uncertain year, but we believe that the Greeks and the Greeks must also know that, although we are with an uncertain health and economic landscape, we count in our community with a stable government, with a solid government, with a responsible government, and that we are going to work every day of this legislation with the firm purpose of making Galicia a place of opportunity for employment -- for equal employment for women and men -- and, above all, a Galicia free from male violence.
